#### Abstracts
##### **Retriever-Free Retrieval: Navigating Documents with Decision Models**
2610.35868v1 by Chao Wan, Paul Jünger, Beixi Du, Bodong Liu, Navaneetha Krishnan, Kilian Q. Weinberger

While language models have become dramatically more capable, retrieval still largely relies on lexical matching or similarity in fixed embedding spaces. This creates a capability asymmetry: what the retriever fails to find, even the strongest generator cannot recover. We propose DecisionRAG, which brings the capabilities of pretrained language models to retrieval using decision models that jointly evaluate candidate branches in a single forward pass, without generating any tokens, and show that judging candidates in a shared context makes their relevance estimates comparable. Like a person browsing a library, DecisionRAG navigates the corpus's inherent structure from broad topics down to individual passages, deciding at each level which branches are worth exploring. Without task-specific training or a first-stage retriever, DecisionRAG outperforms dense retrieval with an 8B reranker by 13.8 R@1 points on MultiDoc2Dial and 17.4 Hit@1 points on Natural Questions. Across four benchmarks, including a corpus of 2.68 million Wikipedia paragraphs, retrieval takes just 0.7-1.8 seconds per query.

摘要：

##### **Center-of-Mass Interpretation of Evolution Strategies**
2610.35865v1 by Akshat Gupta

Evolution strategies (ES) update a model by sampling a population of perturbed copies, scoring each one, and moving in the reward-weighted direction of the perturbations. This update is usually presented as an estimate of a smoothed gradient. We present a geometrical interpretation of this update instead. Treat each perturbed model as a particle and its reward as the particle's mass. Before scoring, every particle has the same mass and the population balances at its centroid. Once rewards are assigned, it balances at its reward-weighted center of mass instead. We show that the mean-subtracted ES update is exactly this displacement, scaled by a scalar factor that depends on the mean reward, for any finite population. The physical reading needs nonnegative rewards, which can always be arranged by adding a constant to every reward without changing the update. In the limit of a large population or under antithetic sampling, the centroid sits on the model itself, so ES has an even simpler geometric interpretation: from its current location, the model moves towards the reward-weighted center of mass of its own population. The same picture explains what subtracting the mean reward does: it changes the point from which the displacement is measured, from the model to the sampled centroid. Our contribution is an intuitive, geometric way to visualize an ES update, exact for any finite population.

摘要：

##### **Patient-specific rubric-based evaluation of large language models across longitudinal cancer care**
2610.35847v1 by Wei-Wei Zhang, Pengkai Wang, Yan Li, Zhijie Sang, Guan-Qun Zhou, Jun-Yan Li, Shu-Han Zhou, Pei-Wen Liu, Ting-Xi Tang, Min Tang, Zhitian Hou, Jianmin Wu, Jian Guan, i Lin, Hongxia Yang, Ying Sun

Large language models (LLMs) show promise in specialty medicine, yet existing evaluations largely focus on isolated clinical encounters rather than tracking how clinical responsibilities evolve across a longitudinal care pathway. Here we introduce NPC-RubricBench, a phase-aware benchmark for evaluating multistage clinical reasoning across the cancer care continuum, using nasopharyngeal carcinoma as a defined disease context. Curated from longitudinal records of 405 patients across two cancer centers, the benchmark comprises 941 patient-phase units across six sequential care phases and 17,688 patient-specific atomic criteria spanning five evaluation dimensions, with each assessment strictly restricted to decision-time clinical evidence. Evaluating 30 general-purpose and medical LLMs revealed that strong diagnostic performance does not carry over to downstream care. We evaluated 30 general-purpose and medical LLMs, showing that high diagnostic performance did not translate into improved downstream care. Instead, the dominant failure modes reversed across phases. Explicit decision errors dominated anatomical staging at 48.2%, whereas decision omissions dominated initial work-up and post-treatment follow-up at 33.4% and 30.4%, respectively. In the evaluated model set, medically adapted models showed higher omission rates than general-purpose models. Moreover, varying query granularity exposes a critical specification trade-off: broad open-ended requests degrade Decision Quality across all 30 models, while more detailed, checklist-like prompts raise Decision Quality but reduce Evidence Faithfulness and Safety Adherence. Furthermore, NPC-RubricBench demonstrates that static diagnostic benchmarks are insufficient to ensure consistent clinical reliability over time, highlighting the importance of phase-adaptive safeguards in multistage medical AI.

摘要：

##### **Preserve to Expand: Source-Aware Adaptation and Model Merging for Multilingual LLM-Based ASR**
2610.35846v1 by Yuchen Zhang, Haralambos Mouratidis, Ravi Shekhar

Expanding multilingual ASR systems to new languages is challenging when adaptation must preserve previously supported languages without replaying source-language training data. We study this problem in connector-based Speech LLMs, where a trainable connector maps speech representations into a frozen large language model. We evaluate target-update construction and merging strategies for adapting the connector while preserving source-language performance. In single-target expansion, target-only fine-tuning and standard TIES merging learn new languages but cause severe source-language forgetting, while SSU-Wanda+TIES gives the best source-preserving trade-off. In multi-target expansion, update merging provides stable source retention, whereas recursive expansion can improve target adaptation under favorable orders but is highly order-sensitive. These results show that multilingual ASR expansion requires balancing target acquisition, source retention, and interactions among target-language updates.

摘要：

##### **I-HITL: Inalienable Human-in-the-Loop**
2610.35835v1 by Samuel Breckenridge, Dani Vilardell, Wenhao Wang, Fan Zhang, Ari Juels

Human oversight is a pillar of AI safety. It is often exercised by a human in the loop (HITL) who approves critical actions taken by an AI agent. AI agents' increasing ability to bypass guardrails, though, raises the question of how to enforce HITL robustly.
  We introduce the concept of inalienable human-in-the-loop (I-HITL). An I-HITL protocol ensures that an agent can initiate an action only with explicit human approval, even when the human interacts with a system (e.g., a local agentic harness) compromised by the agent. A strong I-HITL protocol additionally ensures such approval even when an adversarial human attempts to delegate approvals to an agent.
  To motivate I-HITL, we report on experiments with a popular agentic wallet platform in which raising an agent's spending cap requires HITL approval via emailed one-time passcodes (OTPs). Given access to the user's email and a task blocked by the cap, we find that ten different frontier models will often autonomously raise the cap, harvesting OTPs to bypass a HITL guardrail and surfacing other vulnerabilities.
  We formally define I-HITL using the concept of communication topologies and prove necessary and sufficient conditions for its realization. We show that an I-HITL protocol requires a trusted entity - a trusted computing device (or equivalent) - and characterize which such devices can and cannot realize an I-HITL protocol. We present a prototype I-HITL system called Countersign that can plug into and strengthen existing HITL systems using off-the-shelf consumer mobile devices.

摘要：

##### **Profiling a Frontier Agent with EBench: From Direct Control to Harness with Policy**
2610.35832v1 by Yifei Yao, Yude Zou, Guanbo Jiao, Xing Gao, Ning Gao, Yukai Wang, Jiantong Chen, Zihou Zhu, Xinyu Li, Ningyu Yan, Shuai Wang, Jianming Ma, Haoyu Guo, Weinan Zhang, Chunhua Shen

Frontier multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can control robots without robot-specific training, but how their capabilities compare and interact with post-trained vision-language-action (VLA) and world action model (WAM) policies across manipulation demands remains to be thoroughly evaluated. In this report, we evaluate GPT-6-Astra on EBench as the core of an agent that controls a robot through an agent harness under the same visual and proprioceptive observation protocol as post-trained policies. We study two agent configurations: direct control, in which the agent generates waypoint commands directly, and harness with policy, in which it can also invoke a post-trained VLA or WAM policy for action execution. Under direct control, the agent achieves competitive overall performance relative to post-trained policies. It leads on short-horizon tasks and exhibits the least performance variation across generalization dimensions, whereas it underperforms post-trained policies on long-horizon tasks, tabletop manipulation, and high-precision contact-rich tasks. In the harness with policy, invoking a post-trained VLA or WAM combines the complementary strengths of both components, raising overall task success to the level of the strongest compared post-trained policies while substantially reducing token cost. However, performance remains below the per-task upper bound of the standalone components due to suboptimal policy invocation, fixed-instruction execution without subgoals, and grasp disruption when the agent resumes control. Overall, our study characterizes the capability boundary of a frontier MLLM agent and suggests directions for improving future agent harness design. Project page: https://internrobotics.github.io/EBench-Astra-Tech-Report/

摘要：

##### **Reva: Real-Time Vulnerability Detection through Contextual Probing of Large Language Models**
2610.35829v1 by Amine Lbath, Massih-Reza Amini

A vulnerability costs more to fix the later it is discovered. As coding agents generate code faster than developers can review it, software needs a first line of defense screening each new function in real time. LLM agents have recently become capable of finding real-world vulnerabilities by working as a security expert would: they run analysis tools, explore the surrounding code, test hypotheses and weigh the evidence before forming their own judgement. This reasoning, however, spans many model calls, so that each verdict takes minutes of compute, far too long for real-time screening. Faster detectors, which fine-tune deep learning models or prompt an LLM for a direct verdict, are less accurate: they judge a function without the context an agent gathers, and an LLM's generated verdict is unreliable even when given some of it.
  We present REVA (REal-time Vulnerability Analysis), an AI vulnerability detector that brings the security context and code understanding components behind agentic detectors into a single forward pass of an LLM. For each new function, REVA gives the LLM a pre-computed threat model of its module, lightweight static analysis findings and call-graph information. REVA leverages a trained probe to read the LLM's internal representations of the new function in this context. On PrimeVul's held-out test set, REVA on GLM-5.3-Flash reaches 60.0% accuracy in about one second per function. This matches VulTriage (59.2%), the state-of-the-art agentic detector, run on the same LLM, while being over 250 times faster and less than a tenth as costly. On the same prompt, the probe is 14% to 17% more accurate than the LLM's own direct verdict. Across three open LLMs, REVA is at least as accurate as VulTriage, and about 10% more accurate on two of them. An LLM's internal representations thus encode much of what agentic detectors obtain through reasoning.

摘要：

##### **Understanding Jev-Style System One Models: Does Structure Reduce Selection Bias?**
2610.35826v1 by Mingyang Song, Mao Zheng, Zhiyuan Ma, Xinpeng Zhou

Selection bias in large language models (LLMs) can persist despite instructions to ignore presentation order or prompts that elicit reasoning. Recent efforts to reproduce Jev-style System One behavior have produced a growing family of models that predict probabilities directly over supplied candidates. Yet systematic comparisons of selection bias across Jev versions, Jev reproductions, and generative LLMs remain limited in multi-option tasks. This leaves a central question: does Jev-style decision structure reduce selection bias? We address this question with a simple test that shuffles options while keeping question and candidate content fixed. Across two Jev versions, twelve Jev reproductions, and six generative LLMs, we trace individual answers through eight presentations and compare them with fixed-order repetition. On our primary collection of 1,700 questions from two benchmarks, Jev's accuracy varies by less than two percentage points, yet its cumulative failure set expands by 27--29\% relative to the original order, with first failures continuing into the final presentation. This loss of initial successes is substantially larger than under fixed-order repetition, even for highly repeatable Jev reproductions. Nor does Jev show a general advantage over the LLM baselines in preserving correct choices. Aggregation offers limited protection: voting yields modest gains for Jev, while even unanimous answers are frequently wrong. These results expose a gap between repeatable decisions and decisions that remain correct across equivalent presentations, showing why stable accuracy alone cannot establish resistance to selection bias.

摘要：

##### **Pretraining Knows What, Simulation Teaches How: Compositional Generalization in a Sim-Trained Robot Agent**
2610.35817v1 by Benjamin Pikus, Daniel Saunders, Mircea Mironenco, Salman Mohammadi, Mathew Mathew, Sirou Zhu, Divya Makkar, Xuhua Huang, Qinbo Li, Supreeth Narasimhaswamy, Siyuan Cen, Thomas Neff, Dejia Xu, Shyamal Buch

A robot policy needs to understand what a task is asking for in a scene, and how to move the robot to accomplish that task. To achieve these two skills, we introduce Zero-real Agent Policy (ZAP), a recipe that teaches a small off-the-shelf vision-language model to control a robot via a simple tool harness. We use a pretrained 27B parameter model to bootstrap world understanding and reasoning, followed by supervised fine-tuning and GRPO on simple simulated tasks to teach it how to move the robot. Without any real-world robot data during training, ZAP reaches 96.7% success on real-world tasks, matching GPT-6 Astra in our harness and outperforming the MolmoAct2 VLA (33.3%), while running 3x faster and 17x cheaper than GPT-6 Astra. We demonstrate that ZAP also generalizes to completely new objects, goals, strategies, and language by seating a test tube in a rack (and choosing a different hole in the rack when prompted), handing a test tube between arms mid-air, drawing an X on a tic-tac-toe board, and playing chess. These goals and types of manipulation don't appear in our training data. Furthermore, ZAP also transfers to an unseen embodiment. On a single-arm Franka, ZAP reaches 55.9% on RoboLab-120, 4.9 percentage points ahead of the leading VLA/WAM baseline.

摘要：

##### **Learning When to Commit: Schedule Distillation for Fast Diffusion Language Models**
2610.35804v1 by Aidar Myrzakhan, Zhiqiang Shen

Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) can generate multiple tokens in parallel, but often require many decoding rounds to complete a response. Efficient generation depends not only on which tokens are easy to predict, but also on how committing to them helps predict the remaining positions. A highly predictable token may provide little useful context, while revealing a less predictable token can help several other positions become predictable sooner. We propose dSpark, a new schedule distillation-based framework for learning when to commit tokens for faster diffusion language generation. To achieve this, we construct unmasking schedules that prioritize tokens whose reveal makes other positions easier to predict, reducing uncertainty earlier in decoding. We then distill these schedules into the model to enable faster parallel sampling, with a KL-divergence regularizer to help preserve generation quality. We extensively evaluate our approach across mathematical reasoning and code generation tasks. On GSM8K, our method achieves remarkable 10.12$\times$ and 5.12$\times$ speedups over the various baselines on LLaDA-8B-Instruct and Dream-7B-Instruct, respectively, while maintaining superior task performance. Our code is available at https://github.com/VILA-Lab/dSpark.

摘要：

##### **Game theory of academic effort and hiring in the age of AI**
2610.35802v1 by Saptarshi Pal, Peter S. Park

Many academic disciplines (in particular, mathematics and the theoretical sciences) face difficult questions about how to adapt their practices to the rise of highly capable artificial intelligence (AI). Before AI, a well-written research paper with a strong theoretical result acted as a reliable signal that its authors understood their field in depth. Now that AI is widely accessible, such signals have become cheaper to produce. As a result, they no longer reliably sort academics by their level of understanding. This has direct consequences for academic hiring. The age of AI raises a question for each side of the academic job market. Should an early-career researcher invest in a deep understanding of their field, or instead use AI to build a longer resume with many papers? Should a recruiter favor applicants with fewer papers who pass an interview aimed at probing understanding, or insist on both publication volume and a pass? In this paper, we develop a game-theoretic model of this two-sided dilemma. We classify all pure-strategy equilibria that can arise in this game and use learning simulations to identify the behaviors that populations of applicants and recruiters may learn to adopt. Our analysis provides a framework for reasoning about how academic hiring can adapt to AI and for evaluating practices that preserve the value of deep understanding.

##### **Quantifying Claim Smoothing by LLM Research Agents**
2610.35793v1 by Tiasa Singha Roy, Manya Wadhwa, Greg Durrett

Deep research, or any use of large language models (LLMs) to read a large document collection and produce insights, requires synthesizing information from different sources. In this work, we find that LLMs produce long-form research reports that read as confident syntheses but which fail to accurately reflect the underlying documents. We call this failure smoothing. In collapsing diverse and potentially conflicting sources into a single narrative, the model discards the evidential "jaggedness" of its corpus and asserts conclusions more decisive than the citations can support. We introduce a claim level scoring pipeline, SmoothScore, to quantify this effect. Our pipeline extracts key conclusive claims from model generated reports, then finds evidence relevant to each claim and clusters that evidence along high level aspects. Finally, each claim is scored with respect to the evidence to quantify the degree of smoothing done by the agent. Across 7 systems and 4 datasets, we find that agents consistently produce stronger claims than warranted.

Hence, Atomic Security Concerns could be viewed as a common abstraction layer for the representation of cybersecurity concepts, independently of their domain, nature, or level of granularity. Building upon this perspective, the paper explores how Atomic Security Concerns may provide the vocabulary of a cybersecurity language and how cybersecurity concepts may be represented as concern sets supporting explicit relationships among concepts. Multivariate time series produced by industrial installations and server clusters are under continuous monitoring, and anomaly detection is the first line of defense before a local fault escalates into a system-wide failure. In the absence of labels, the dominant paradigm trains a reconstruction model on normal data and scores the residual to reach a verdict. Recent work has steadily enriched the expressiveness of the recovery map through Transformers, diffusion models, and cross-scale associations. A residual, however, only certifies that an observation can be recovered; it does not certify that the variables still explain one another as they do under normal operation. A coordinated yet unfamiliar operating transition inflates the residual without breaking any relationship, whereas a sensor drifting away from its peers may leave the residual silent. Methods that fold inter-variable dependencies into the reconstruction objective inherit this ambiguity, because their relational evidence is ultimately supervised by the same recovery loss. We therefore propose CLOSURE, which evaluates recoverability and cross-channel consistency as two normal-data prediction tasks learned in separation. The recoverability task is solved by a diffusion reconstructor conditioned on a discrete memory of normal temporal bases. The consistency task is solved by a relation-closure operator that predicts every channel exclusively from the others, where the target's own trajectory is withheld and sparse signed gates decide which peers are actually used. The two scores are reconciled only through calibration on normal data. Extensive experiments show that CLOSURE consistently outperforms recent reconstruction-, frequency-, and topology-based detectors on public benchmark streams, confirming the value of examining consistency alongside recoverability.

##### **VaLoP: Value Gradient Lookahead and Pullback for Reward-Guided Diffusion**
2610.35607v1 by Kwanyoung Kim

Reward guidance steers a pretrained diffusion or flow model toward images or actions that a reward model prefers, at sampling time and without retraining the model for each reward. Interestingly, we find that both errors are posterior covariances, which grow with the uncertainty about the clean sample, and hence at higher noise. To reduce them, we evaluate the value at a less noisy state, one sampler step ahead, where this uncertainty is smaller, and pull its gradient back to the current state. We therefore propose Value Gradient Lookahead and Pullback (VaLoP), which computes this correction with one vector-Jacobian product, and VaLoP-Fast, which amortizes it with a small head that, for text-to-image models, is trained with a single reward and reused for the others. Across various models, rewards, and benchmark datasets, both robustly outperform existing value-gradient guidance on the target reward, VaLoP-Fast at about the cost of learned-value guidance. To remove a distractor's effect without knowing which sentence it is, we propose EXCISE, an inference-time method that blocks the sentence most influencing the answer, but only if the model's hidden states mark it as inconsistent with the case. On MedQA-TD (MedQA with Targeted Distractors), a benchmark we construct in which each distractor targets a specific wrong option, EXCISE gains 16.7-22.8 accuracy points at a 1.5-2.1-point cost on the same questions without distractors, outperforming prompting, rewriting, and steering baselines for all three models. Its gains extend to MedDistractQA and BiasMedQA, as well as chain-of-thought generation.

##### **LoopFold: Parallelizing Recurrent Depth in Looped Transformers**
2610.35556v1 by Feichen Song, Boyi Zeng, Yiqin Hao, Shixiang Song, He Li, Yizhou Yang, Zitong Wang, Yifan Liu, Ziwei He, Zhouhan Lin

Looped Transformers increase computation per token by repeatedly applying a shared backbone, offering a way to improve language modeling and downstream task performance at a fixed backbone parameter count. However, each loop depends on the preceding loop, so increasing recurrent depth also increases decoding latency. Existing approaches include Parallel Loop Transformers, which reduce latency by overlapping loop computation across token positions, but yield rapidly diminishing marginal accuracy gains at larger depths. To address this challenge, we introduce LoopFold, which folds computation at multiple recurrent depths into parallel batches for the same token prefix. A lightweight predictor estimates inputs to later loops so they can start before earlier loops finish. A corrector then refines the outputs of these later loops using newly available results from earlier loops. All components are trained jointly using only the final next-token prediction loss. In experiments with 1.4B models, deeper LoopFold configurations achieve progressively higher average zero-shot accuracy across eight tasks, rising from 53.41% to 56.38%. Vanilla looped models and PLT show limited gains over the same depth range. Compared with a nine-loop vanilla model, LoopFold uses five sequential stages and additional parallel computation to reduce decoding latency by 46%.

##### **Cross-Domain Sequential Recommendation with Representation-Level User Transfer**
2610.35550v1 by Abdulaziz Samra, Nikita Sukhorukov, Alexey Grishanov, Alexey Vasilev, Evgeny Frolov

Cross-domain sequential recommendation aims to improve next-item prediction in a target domain by leveraging user behavior from auxiliary source domains. However, real-world deployment faces two critical challenges: business constraints that isolate interaction data across domains, and user heterogeneity, where many users lack cross-domain activity. This makes existing cross-domain methods difficult to apply. In this paper, we propose a simple and practical framework for cross-domain sequential recommendations that transfers information exclusively through learned user representations, without sharing raw interaction data or item embeddings across domains. We introduce a lightweight adapter that projects the source-domain user representation into the target-domain embedding space and integrates it with the target interaction sequence. Importantly, when source data is unavailable (i.e., the user observed only in the target domain), the model seamlessly defaults to a robust target-only sequential recommender, retaining benefits from joint training across all users. Extensive experiments were conducted under two setups; one targets benchmarking against previous baselines, while the other focuses on realistic and scalable conditions. Both sets of experiments demonstrate consistent performance gains for shared and unshared users.

##### **StateLoop: Persistent State for Looped Transformers**
2610.35544v1 by Feichen Song, Boyi Zeng, He Li, Shixiang Song, Yiqin Hao, Zitong Wang, Yizhou Yang, Yifan Liu, Ziwei He, Zhouhan Lin

Looped Transformers offer a parameter-efficient approach to language modeling by repeatedly applying a shared backbone. Vanilla looped Transformers use each backbone output directly as the input to the next iteration. These outputs may contain information useful several iterations later, but retaining it across the loop depends entirely on the single hidden state. We introduce StateLoop, which maintains one additional persistent state per token to retain information across iterations. At each iteration, a learned gate controls how much of the previous state is retained and how much information from the backbone output is incorporated. The updated state is projected back and added to the backbone output for subsequent iterations and final prediction. This design reduces Pile perplexity and improves average accuracy across eight zero-shot tasks over Vanilla Looped. Its perplexity advantage over Vanilla Looped grows with trained loop depth, consistent with persistent state becoming increasingly useful as recurrent computation deepens.

##### **An Algorithmic Account of Few-Shot Concept Induction in Language Models**
2610.35533v1 by Michael A. Lepori, Laila Johnston, Aalok Sathe, Zhuonan Yang, Roman Feiman, Ellie Pavlick

Both models and minds not only encode features that describe an entity, but can specifically represent which features of that entity are relevant for a given context. This core capability underlies in-context learning in language models and a variety of cognitive phenomena in minds, yet it is unclear how intelligent systems identify and promote relevant features. In this work, we propose Amortized Direction Accentuation (ADA), an algorithmic hypothesis that provides an explanation for how relevance is determined in the context of few-shot concept induction --- the ability to infer a latent concept from a small number of positive examples. Under ADA, concept induction proceeds by forming a summary representation of the examples in a feature space that is shaped by amortized knowledge about which features tend to be useful for finding commonalities between items. We investigate and validate ADA in language models using a few-shot concept induction task. Built on Envoy, it splits work by latency profile: I/O-bound control decisions (authentication, credential brokering, policy and budget evaluation) run in an out-of-process external-processing service, while wire-format translation, token metering and prompt-cache shaping run in-proxy as a Rust WebAssembly filter, so that, by default, streamed response body chunks never wait on an out-of-process call. ##### **The Hidden Costs of Forgetting: A Study of Trajectory Deletion in Software Engineering Agents**
2610.35496v1 by Fernando Vallecillos-Ruiz, Islem Bouzenia, Michael Pradel, Leon Moonen

Software engineering agents resend their entire trajectory to an LLM at every turn, motivating online trajectory reduction. Prior work shows that randomly deleting 75% of tokens in old turns may preserve agent effectiveness, but it remains unclear why, how agents compensate for missing content, and whether it actually reduces costs. This paper presents an empirical study of 42 trajectory-reduction configurations, focusing on effectiveness, agent behavior, and cost. We systematically explore deleting content from old turns by varying the scope, granularity, amount, and timing of deletion across 9,400 runs with two state-of-the-art agents on SWE-bench Verified. We find deleting large amounts of trajectory content (e.g., up to 100% of reasoning text) can preserve overall effectiveness. Importantly, such deletion must be applied with a delay, as immediate deletion tends to significantly degrade effectiveness. Agents often compensate for deleted content by re-fetching it, increasing both the number of turns taken and the input/output tokens. Whether trajectory reduction reduces costs depends on the pricing model: Under a flat pricing model, estimated costs fall in 26 of 42 evaluations. Surprisingly, under a caching-aware pricing model, zero evaluations yield lower costs, since reducing the trajectory breaks the cached prefix billed at a lower rate. Our findings have three implications: First, token counts alone are misleading; trajectory reduction techniques must consider overall costs under a realistic pricing model. Second, analyzing trajectories is crucial to understand agent behavior, as end-to-end effectiveness hides differences such as excessive re-fetching. Finally, these results highlight the need for future work on memory management to reduce costs without sacrificing effectiveness.

Clinical expertise is inherently distributed: it resides not in any single dataset or institution but in the accumulated reasoning experiences of thousands of clinicians navigating diverse patient populations, resource constraints, and evolving treatment protocols. Without a way to distinguish agents from other automated clients such as simple bots and scripts, these services risk large-scale automated junk submissions that waste service resources and consume users' attention or tokens. In this paper, we investigate a novel question for today's Web: "How can a Web service obtain evidence that a client is an agent?" We introduce the concept of "Proof-of-Agentness" (PoA), a mechanism for distinguishing agents from other automated clients, motivated by the parallel between human attention and an agent's inference resources. We outline two approaches: active verification through challenge-response and passive analysis of service-visible signals. Eight language models achieve an overall accuracy of 96.5% on our challenges, with an average overhead of 1,647 tokens. For passive analysis, we conduct an IRB-approved user study comparing the Web interactions of twelve human participants and representative Bot and Agent implementations, achieving an average classification accuracy of 95.8% across four classifiers, motivating further study.

摘要：

##### **Satellite Intelligence: Distributed Training, Collaborative Inference,and LLM Adaptation in LEO Constellation**
2610.35282v1 by Zheng Lin, Zhanwei Wang, Yuxin Zhang, Yafei Wang, Ons Aouedi, Zihan Fang, Jinfeng Xu, Jing Yang, Vu Nguyen Ha, Yuanwei Liu, Mohamed-Slim Alouini, Symeon Chatzinotas

The proliferation of orbital sensing data and the real-time demands of emerging global services are pushing low Earth orbit (LEO) satellite networks from bent-pipe relays toward intelligent onboard processing. This shift confronts a fundamental tension, where modern artificial intelligence (AI) training paradigms require intensive computation, abundant memory, and stable connectivity, all of which are scarce under the dynamic topologies, intermittent links, and severe size, weight, and power (SWaP) constraints of satellite networks. Addressing this tension demands rethinking AI workflows, from training and inference to the emerging deployment of large language models (LLMs), under a constraint-aware design philosophy. To the best of our knowledge, this paper provides the first survey that jointly covers all three, whereas prior surveys examine these aspects in isolation. We begin by identifying the deployment challenge of satellite intelligence (SI) and deriving design principles for SI. We then examine three advancing paradigms: i) distributed training, where federated learning (FL) and split learning (SL) enable satellite-ground co-training under resource limitations; ii) collaborative inference, which exploits single-satellite, multi-satellite, and satellite-ground architectures to meet real-time service demands; and iii) on-orbit LLM adaptation and serving, which tackles the frontier challenge of deploying LLMs under severe orbital constraints and resource-aware serving. Finally, we identify open challenges and future directions. By bridging the AI and satellite networks, this paper provides a research roadmap for the implementation of space-native AI technologies.

摘要：
